.msb

Based on the provided hex dumps and file paths, the .msb format is an EA Pathfinder Interactive Music Data Bank used to govern dynamic audio routing and playback logic in titles like The Simpsons Game.

Format Structure & Breakdown

  • File Linkage Header: The first 0x50 bytes act as a wrapper that directly references the paired audio stream file containing the actual music data. You can see the target filename encoded in plaintext right before the main data chunk (e.g., menu_mus.mus, loc_mus.mus, brt_mus.mus).
  • Pathfinder Magic Number: At exact offset 0x50, the chunk identifier PFDx appears, signaling the start of the Electronic Arts Pathfinder engine data.
  • Binary Routing Logic: Directly following PFDx is the compiled state machine. This area contains arrays of sequential bytes (e.g., 01 02 01 07 01 0D) and memory offsets that map out node transitions, branching paths, and playback conditions for the interactive music engine.
  • Plaintext Metadata Trailer: The file concludes with a highly readable symbol table that acts as an index for the game engine to interface with the audio logic. This section is split into three distinct categories:
    • Events (numevents): Lists triggerable interactive cues (like fe_start, stop, puzzle1) mapped to their hashed integer IDs (e.g., stop:22419055).
    • Entry Nodes (numparts): Maps logical musical phases to exact entry points within the binary state machine (e.g., menu1 entry node = 41 or loc_hz entry node = 0).
    • Definitions (numdefines): Sets core configuration constants. This includes the track's memory ID (PATH_TRACK_menu_mus 0x11000001), the expected byte rate for timing (PATH_TRACK_BYTESPERSEC), and global bitmasks for system broadcasts (e.g., PATH_ALL_VOICES F0000000 and PATH_INTENSITY 0).

In short, the .msb file acts as the conductor's sheet music: it holds no actual audio itself, but rather feeds the game engine a list of triggers, a node-based roadmap of how the tracks should seamlessly loop and transition, and a direct pointer to the .mus file containing the audio payload.
